        Project directory:

        Idea:
Design a robust fix for stale pinnedShotGroup.trackId in the video editor. Context: pinnedShotGroup.trackId is denormalized state that says which track a group's clips live on, but it can become stale when clips move across tracks. We just fixed the most critical data-loss bug (clips silently deleted on drag) in multi-drag-utils.ts, but 26 of 38 usages of group.trackId are vulnerable. The audit found 11 HIGH RISK (data loss) and 15 MEDIUM RISK (silent failures) call sites across usePinnedShotGroups.ts, useTimelineTrackManagement.ts, useSwitchToFinalVideo.ts, shot-group-commands.ts, and others. Two approaches: (1) defensive helper that resolves actual trackId from clip positions at each call site, or (2) ensure trackId is updated atomically whenever clips move. Need a plan that's robust but not over-engineered — this is a video editor timeline, not a distributed system.

  "meta_commentary": "Execution guidance:\n\n1. **Start with T1 (helpers)**. These are pure functions with no side effects \u2014 easy to get right and test. The key design decisions: resolveGroupTrackId must check the stored-trackId row FIRST (fast path) before scanning all rows. Use a Set of clipIds for O(1) membership checks. Return the row with the most matching clips when split across rows. Fall back to stored trackId (not undefined) for orphaned groups.\n\n2. **T3/T5/T7 are the highest-risk tasks.** T5 (sync loop) is the most complex \u2014 carefully count all 8 row-access references vs 2 identity-matching references. Read the actual current code before editing; line numbers in the plan may have drifted slightly from recent commits.\n\n3. **T3-T4 and T5-T9 can be parallelized** since they touch different files. But T5 deserves the most careful review.\n\n4. **Self-healing pattern**: When any function finds a group via resolution (meaning resolvedTrackId != storedTrackId), the OUTPUT mutation must include `trackId: resolvedTrackId` in the group object. This is how staleness gets repaired on contact.\n\n5. **Don't touch useShotGroupHandlers.ts** (FLAG-002). It's a minor issue where stale-video badges use `group.trackId` \u2014 but fixing it via the useShotGroups.ts display hook (T6) partially addresses this since ShotGroup.rowId will now be correct. The staleShotGroupIds set in useShotGroupHandlers still uses raw group.trackId, but fixing that is out of scope per the plan.\n\n6. **Import paths**: Both new helpers import from the same file they're defined in, so no circular dependency risk. Callers in hooks/ and lib/ already import from pinned-group-projection.ts.\n\n7. **Type signature for resolveGroupTrackId**: `(group: PinnedShotGroup, rows: TimelineRow[]) => string`. Always returns a string (falls back to group.trackId). For findGroupForTrack: `(groups: PinnedShotGroup[], shotId: string, trackId: string, rows: TimelineRow[]) => PinnedShotGroup | undefined`.\n\n8. **The categorizeSelection function** in pinned-group-projection.ts returns `PinnedGroupKey` objects with `{ shotId, trackId }`. These keys come from stored group state and may be stale. The plan addresses this in T7 (moveSelectedClipsToTrack) by using findGroupForTrack to look up the actual group from a potentially-stale key.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-projection.ts\n@@\n const getGroupId = (groupKey: PinnedGroupKey): string => `${groupKey.shotId}:${groupKey.trackId}`;\n+\n+const countMatchingClipIds = (row: TimelineRow | undefined, clipIds: readonly string[]): number => {\n+  if (!row || clipIds.length === 0) {\n+    return 0;\n+  }\n+\n+  const rowClipIds = new Set(row.actions.map((action) => action.id));\n+  let matches = 0;\n+  for (const clipId of clipIds) {\n+    if (rowClipIds.has(clipId)) {\n+      matches += 1;\n+    }\n+  }\n+\n+  return matches;\n+};\n+\n+export function resolveGroupTrackId(group: PinnedShotGroup, rows: TimelineRow[]): string {\n+  const storedRow = rows.find((row) => row.id === group.trackId);\n+  const storedMatchCount = countMatchingClipIds(storedRow, group.clipIds);\n+\n+  // Fast path: every live clip still sits on the stored row.\n+  if (storedMatchCount === group.clipIds.length && storedMatchCount > 0) {\n+    return group.trackId;\n+  }\n+\n+  let bestTrackId = storedMatchCount > 0 ? group.trackId : '';\n+  let bestMatchCount = storedMatchCount;\n+\n+  for (const row of rows) {\n+    if (row.id === group.trackId) {\n+      continue;\n+    }\n+\n+    const matchCount = countMatchingClipIds(row, group.clipIds);\n+    if (matchCount > bestMatchCount) {\n+      bestMatchCount = matchCount;\n+      bestTrackId = row.id;\n+    }\n+  }\n+\n+  return bestMatchCount > 0 ? bestTrackId : group.trackId;\n+}\n+\n+export function findGroupForTrack(\n+  groups: PinnedShotGroup[],\n+  shotId: string,\n+  trackId: string,\n+  rows: TimelineRow[],\n+): PinnedShotGroup | undefined {\n+  const exactMatch = groups.find((group) => group.shotId === shotId && group.trackId === trackId);\n+  if (exactMatch && resolveGroupTrackId(exactMatch, rows) === trackId) {\n+    return exactMatch;\n+  }\n+\n+  return groups.find(\n+    (group) => group.shotId === shotId && resolveGroupTrackId(group, rows) === trackId,\n+  );\n+}\n \n /**\n  * Find the pinned group that contains `clipId`, if any. Soft-tag lookup only —\n  * returns the first group whose `clipIds` list includes the target.\n  */\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Design a robust fix for stale pinnedShotGroup.trackId in the video editor. Context: pinnedShotGroup.trackId is denormalized state that says which track a group's clips live on, but it can become stale when clips move across tracks. We just fixed the most critical data-loss bug (clips silently deleted on drag) in multi-drag-utils.ts, but 26 of 38 usages of group.trackId are vulnerable. The audit found 11 HIGH RISK (data loss) and 15 MEDIUM RISK (silent failures) call sites across usePinnedShotGroups.ts, useTimelineTrackManagement.ts, useSwitchToFinalVideo.ts, shot-group-commands.ts, and others. Two approaches: (1) defensive helper that resolves actual trackId from clip positions at each call site, or (2) ensure trackId is updated atomically whenever clips move. Need a plan that's robust but not over-engineered — this is a video editor timeline, not a distributed system.

  "meta_commentary": "Execution guidance:\n\n1. **Start with T1 (helpers)**. These are pure functions with no side effects \u2014 easy to get right and test. The key design decisions: resolveGroupTrackId must check the stored-trackId row FIRST (fast path) before scanning all rows. Use a Set of clipIds for O(1) membership checks. Return the row with the most matching clips when split across rows. Fall back to stored trackId (not undefined) for orphaned groups.\n\n2. **T3/T5/T7 are the highest-risk tasks.** T5 (sync loop) is the most complex \u2014 carefully count all 8 row-access references vs 2 identity-matching references. Read the actual current code before editing; line numbers in the plan may have drifted slightly from recent commits.\n\n3. **T3-T4 and T5-T9 can be parallelized** since they touch different files. But T5 deserves the most careful review.\n\n4. **Self-healing pattern**: When any function finds a group via resolution (meaning resolvedTrackId != storedTrackId), the OUTPUT mutation must include `trackId: resolvedTrackId` in the group object. This is how staleness gets repaired on contact.\n\n5. **Don't touch useShotGroupHandlers.ts** (FLAG-002). It's a minor issue where stale-video badges use `group.trackId` \u2014 but fixing it via the useShotGroups.ts display hook (T6) partially addresses this since ShotGroup.rowId will now be correct. The staleShotGroupIds set in useShotGroupHandlers still uses raw group.trackId, but fixing that is out of scope per the plan.\n\n6. **Import paths**: Both new helpers import from the same file they're defined in, so no circular dependency risk. Callers in hooks/ and lib/ already import from pinned-group-projection.ts.\n\n7. **Type signature for resolveGroupTrackId**: `(group: PinnedShotGroup, rows: TimelineRow[]) => string`. Always returns a string (falls back to group.trackId). For findGroupForTrack: `(groups: PinnedShotGroup[], shotId: string, trackId: string, rows: TimelineRow[]) => PinnedShotGroup | undefined`.\n\n8. **The categorizeSelection function** in pinned-group-projection.ts returns `PinnedGroupKey` objects with `{ shotId, trackId }`. These keys come from stored group state and may be stale. The plan addresses this in T7 (moveSelectedClipsToTrack) by using findGroupForTrack to look up the actual group from a potentially-stale key.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised grouped-drag plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still leaves `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` stale on cross-track moves. the data model stores `trackid`, and current drag/move helpers only update rows/meta/clip order, so a pinned group dragged to another track would keep pointing at the old track unless the drag commit also patches group metadata. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 2.4 against `materializedata()` in `usetimelinecommit` and `assembletimelinedata()` in `timeline-data.ts`. the plan still says to overwrite `pinnedshotgroups` after `materializedata()` returns, but `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so mutating the config afterward would leave signature-derived state stale unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the grouped-drag plan against `useclipdrag` and `usetimelinetrackmanagement.movecliptorow()`. the plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still does not say how `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` is updated when a grouped drag or move lands on a different row, so the overlay and menu state can still point at the old track after a successful cross-track move. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the cross-track multi-drag new-track branch in `useclipdrag`. that path still commits through `applyedit({ type: 'config', resolvedconfig })` using `buildconfigfromdragresult()`, and the revised plan does not explain how pinned-group metadata is updated atomically on that path, so grouped drags that create a new track are still under-specified even beyond the stale-`trackid` problem. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Design a robust fix for stale pinnedShotGroup.trackId in the video editor. Context: pinnedShotGroup.trackId is denormalized state that says which track a group's clips live on, but it can become stale when clips move across tracks. We just fixed the most critical data-loss bug (clips silently deleted on drag) in multi-drag-utils.ts, but 26 of 38 usages of group.trackId are vulnerable. The audit found 11 HIGH RISK (data loss) and 15 MEDIUM RISK (silent failures) call sites across usePinnedShotGroups.ts, useTimelineTrackManagement.ts, useSwitchToFinalVideo.ts, shot-group-commands.ts, and others. Two approaches: (1) defensive helper that resolves actual trackId from clip positions at each call site, or (2) ensure trackId is updated atomically whenever clips move. Need a plan that's robust but not over-engineered — this is a video editor timeline, not a distributed system.

  "meta_commentary": "Execution guidance:\n\n1. **Start with T1 (helpers)**. These are pure functions with no side effects \u2014 easy to get right and test. The key design decisions: resolveGroupTrackId must check the stored-trackId row FIRST (fast path) before scanning all rows. Use a Set of clipIds for O(1) membership checks. Return the row with the most matching clips when split across rows. Fall back to stored trackId (not undefined) for orphaned groups.\n\n2. **T3/T5/T7 are the highest-risk tasks.** T5 (sync loop) is the most complex \u2014 carefully count all 8 row-access references vs 2 identity-matching references. Read the actual current code before editing; line numbers in the plan may have drifted slightly from recent commits.\n\n3. **T3-T4 and T5-T9 can be parallelized** since they touch different files. But T5 deserves the most careful review.\n\n4. **Self-healing pattern**: When any function finds a group via resolution (meaning resolvedTrackId != storedTrackId), the OUTPUT mutation must include `trackId: resolvedTrackId` in the group object. This is how staleness gets repaired on contact.\n\n5. **Don't touch useShotGroupHandlers.ts** (FLAG-002). It's a minor issue where stale-video badges use `group.trackId` \u2014 but fixing it via the useShotGroups.ts display hook (T6) partially addresses this since ShotGroup.rowId will now be correct. The staleShotGroupIds set in useShotGroupHandlers still uses raw group.trackId, but fixing that is out of scope per the plan.\n\n6. **Import paths**: Both new helpers import from the same file they're defined in, so no circular dependency risk. Callers in hooks/ and lib/ already import from pinned-group-projection.ts.\n\n7. **Type signature for resolveGroupTrackId**: `(group: PinnedShotGroup, rows: TimelineRow[]) => string`. Always returns a string (falls back to group.trackId). For findGroupForTrack: `(groups: PinnedShotGroup[], shotId: string, trackId: string, rows: TimelineRow[]) => PinnedShotGroup | undefined`.\n\n8. **The categorizeSelection function** in pinned-group-projection.ts returns `PinnedGroupKey` objects with `{ shotId, trackId }`. These keys come from stored group state and may be stale. The plan addresses this in T7 (moveSelectedClipsToTrack) by using findGroupForTrack to look up the actual group from a potentially-stale key.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order.         Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Design a robust fix for stale pinnedShotGroup.trackId in the video editor. Context: pinnedShotGroup.trackId is denormalized state that says which track a group's clips live on, but it can become stale when clips move across tracks. We just fixed the most critical data-loss bug (clips silently deleted on drag) in multi-drag-utils.ts, but 26 of 38 usages of group.trackId are vulnerable. The audit found 11 HIGH RISK (data loss) and 15 MEDIUM RISK (silent failures) call sites across usePinnedShotGroups.ts, useTimelineTrackManagement.ts, useSwitchToFinalVideo.ts, shot-group-commands.ts, and others. Two approaches: (1) defensive helper that resolves actual trackId from clip positions at each call site, or (2) ensure trackId is updated atomically whenever clips move. Need a plan that's robust but not over-engineered — this is a video editor timeline, not a distributed system.

    "description": "Add `resolveGroupTrackId(group, rows)` and `findGroupForTrack(groups, shotId, trackId, rows)` helpers to `src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`. resolveGroupTrackId: fast path checks stored trackId row for matching clipIds (O(1) row + O(clips)); slow path scans all rows (O(rows\u00d7clips)); falls back to stored trackId if no clips found. findGroupForTrack: exact (shotId, trackId) match first, then fallback to resolveGroupTrackId scan. Export both alongside existing exports.",
    "depends_on": [],
    "status": "done",
    "executor_notes": "Added `resolveGroupTrackId(group, rows)` and `findGroupForTrack(groups, shotId, trackId, rows)` in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`. `resolveGroupTrackId` checks the stored row first, returns immediately when all group clips still live there, otherwise scans rows and returns the row with the most matching clipIds, falling back to the stored `trackId` when no live clips are found. `findGroupForTrack` now preserves composite `(shotId, trackId)` identity by only accepting an exact stored-key match if it still resolves to the requested track, then falling back to a resolved scan across same-shot groups. Verification: baseline `npx vitest run src/tools/video-editor/` passed before edits; post-change `npx vitest run src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-projection.test.ts` and `npx vitest run src/tools/video-editor/` both passed.",

  "meta_commentary": "Execution guidance:\n\n1. **Start with T1 (helpers)**. These are pure functions with no side effects \u2014 easy to get right and test. The key design decisions: resolveGroupTrackId must check the stored-trackId row FIRST (fast path) before scanning all rows. Use a Set of clipIds for O(1) membership checks. Return the row with the most matching clips when split across rows. Fall back to stored trackId (not undefined) for orphaned groups.\n\n2. **T3/T5/T7 are the highest-risk tasks.** T5 (sync loop) is the most complex \u2014 carefully count all 8 row-access references vs 2 identity-matching references. Read the actual current code before editing; line numbers in the plan may have drifted slightly from recent commits.\n\n3. **T3-T4 and T5-T9 can be parallelized** since they touch different files. But T5 deserves the most careful review.\n\n4. **Self-healing pattern**: When any function finds a group via resolution (meaning resolvedTrackId != storedTrackId), the OUTPUT mutation must include `trackId: resolvedTrackId` in the group object. This is how staleness gets repaired on contact.\n\n5. **Don't touch useShotGroupHandlers.ts** (FLAG-002). It's a minor issue where stale-video badges use `group.trackId` \u2014 but fixing it via the useShotGroups.ts display hook (T6) partially addresses this since ShotGroup.rowId will now be correct. The staleShotGroupIds set in useShotGroupHandlers still uses raw group.trackId, but fixing that is out of scope per the plan.\n\n6. **Import paths**: Both new helpers import from the same file they're defined in, so no circular dependency risk. Callers in hooks/ and lib/ already import from pinned-group-projection.ts.\n\n7. **Type signature for resolveGroupTrackId**: `(group: PinnedShotGroup, rows: TimelineRow[]) => string`. Always returns a string (falls back to group.trackId). For findGroupForTrack: `(groups: PinnedShotGroup[], shotId: string, trackId: string, rows: TimelineRow[]) => PinnedShotGroup | undefined`.\n\n8. **The categorizeSelection function** in pinned-group-projection.ts returns `PinnedGroupKey` objects with `{ shotId, trackId }`. These keys come from stored group state and may be stale. The plan addresses this in T7 (moveSelectedClipsToTrack) by using findGroupForTrack to look up the actual group from a potentially-stale key.",

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Design a robust fix for stale pinnedShotGroup.trackId in the video editor. Context: pinnedShotGroup.trackId is denormalized state that says which track a group's clips live on, but it can become stale when clips move across tracks. We just fixed the most critical data-loss bug (clips silently deleted on drag) in multi-drag-utils.ts, but 26 of 38 usages of group.trackId are vulnerable. The audit found 11 HIGH RISK (data loss) and 15 MEDIUM RISK (silent failures) call sites across usePinnedShotGroups.ts, useTimelineTrackManagement.ts, useSwitchToFinalVideo.ts, shot-group-commands.ts, and others. Two approaches: (1) defensive helper that resolves actual trackId from clip positions at each call site, or (2) ensure trackId is updated atomically whenever clips move. Need a plan that's robust but not over-engineered — this is a video editor timeline, not a distributed system.

  "meta_commentary": "Execution guidance:\n\n1. **Start with T1 (helpers)**. These are pure functions with no side effects \u2014 easy to get right and test. The key design decisions: resolveGroupTrackId must check the stored-trackId row FIRST (fast path) before scanning all rows. Use a Set of clipIds for O(1) membership checks. Return the row with the most matching clips when split across rows. Fall back to stored trackId (not undefined) for orphaned groups.\n\n2. **T3/T5/T7 are the highest-risk tasks.** T5 (sync loop) is the most complex \u2014 carefully count all 8 row-access references vs 2 identity-matching references. Read the actual current code before editing; line numbers in the plan may have drifted slightly from recent commits.\n\n3. **T3-T4 and T5-T9 can be parallelized** since they touch different files. But T5 deserves the most careful review.\n\n4. **Self-healing pattern**: When any function finds a group via resolution (meaning resolvedTrackId != storedTrackId), the OUTPUT mutation must include `trackId: resolvedTrackId` in the group object. This is how staleness gets repaired on contact.\n\n5. **Don't touch useShotGroupHandlers.ts** (FLAG-002). It's a minor issue where stale-video badges use `group.trackId` \u2014 but fixing it via the useShotGroups.ts display hook (T6) partially addresses this since ShotGroup.rowId will now be correct. The staleShotGroupIds set in useShotGroupHandlers still uses raw group.trackId, but fixing that is out of scope per the plan.\n\n6. **Import paths**: Both new helpers import from the same file they're defined in, so no circular dependency risk. Callers in hooks/ and lib/ already import from pinned-group-projection.ts.\n\n7. **Type signature for resolveGroupTrackId**: `(group: PinnedShotGroup, rows: TimelineRow[]) => string`. Always returns a string (falls back to group.trackId). For findGroupForTrack: `(groups: PinnedShotGroup[], shotId: string, trackId: string, rows: TimelineRow[]) => PinnedShotGroup | undefined`.\n\n8. **The categorizeSelection function** in pinned-group-projection.ts returns `PinnedGroupKey` objects with `{ shotId, trackId }`. These keys come from stored group state and may be stale. The plan addresses this in T7 (moveSelectedClipsToTrack) by using findGroupForTrack to look up the actual group from a potentially-stale key.",
